# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, use the MCP server's `load_workspace_dependencies` tool (`mcp__codex_app__load_workspace_dependencies`) to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to conversations in Codex. Use "chat" when referring to conversations in the product. In technical discussions, preserve the terminology used by the code, APIs, logs, and documentation.
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.

## User Profile

Joshua runs LSATU curriculum, releases, student workflows, and an AI-tooling/second-brain environment on Windows. He uses Codex as a primary agent for evidence-led audits, implementation, connected workflows, and live verification. Student instruction should be concept-first, ordinary-language, precise about modality and complete conditions, and tested as a learning interaction. He is configuring Orca as a multi-agent workspace with Codex primary.

## User preferences

- Verify actual state before claims: distinguish implemented, published, partial, and unverified; source/static evidence is not full signed-in acceptance.
- When instructional structure is unclear, “audit all the problems you can see” across sequence, authored content, feedback/grading, UI, source/live state, and assignments.
- Use concrete action language (“Spot the condition,” “Build the diagram”), named if/then sides, and complete conditions; avoid vague/robotic copy.
- Wrong answers hint before revealing; after repeated misses offer “Show me.” Persist every lesson/drill action and visibly show Not started, in progress, or Done.
- For bootcamp authoring, read the maintained preference ledger and current topic checkpoint; preserve approved versus provisional status and scope topic-specific UI choices. [ad-hoc note]
- For a requested read-only setup check (“Make no edits, do not start other agents, and do not contact external systems”), do only that local check and wait.
- Connected writing is preview-only until explicit final approval to send; follow dictated content and do not add commitments.
- Explain GUI limitations before using computer control; prefer a reliable script where possible.

## General Tips

- Read C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\AGENTS.md before AI OS work: wiki/hot.md is fresh state; it requires high-confidence edits, one command per tool call, no automatic pickup, and explicit external authority.
- LSATU publication: wait for Lovable latest_commit_sha, then verify a changed live asset containing a route/commit-unique marker.
- Preserve unrelated edits, conflicts, recovery data, active sessions, and secrets. Never retain exposed keys.
- Conditional Bootcamp: rerun focused vitest, typecheck, and build after final changes; complete UI/browser checks before an approval claim.
- Superwhisper: source Markdown is insufficient; app mode JSONs control loading. Run sync.py --restart and read live settings/counts. Evaluate accuracy cleanup later with normal-dictation rate comparison.
- Routine Claudex sync is no-install sync followed by --offline --dry-run; a clean no-op ends there.

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: target 34 one-to-one sessions a week
plus consultations (calendar showed ~13/week, 2026-07 to 2026-10). Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

## Shared Windows CLI tools

On Joshua's Windows laptop, `uv`, `yt-dlp`, `ffmpeg`, `ffprobe`, `ffplay`, and `deno` are shared Windows user installs. Read `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\references\ai-workspace\windows-cli-toolchain.md` for their paths, versions, usage, and PATH refresh steps. Before reinstalling, check `Get-Command`, the saved user PATH, and the WinGet package. A running IDE may have stale PATH; refresh the shell or restart that IDE. Keep Python dependencies in project environments.

<!-- HAND-OWNED since 2026-07-17. This file is the authority for Codex — edit it directly. On any major change, mirror it into CLAUDE.md so Claude and Codex stay in sync. -->
# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| LLM Wiki schema (source, page, ingest/query/lint rules) | `system/schema/llm-wiki.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Shared Windows CLI tools for Codex, Claude Code, and Antigravity | `references/ai-workspace/windows-cli-toolchain.md` — installed paths, PATH refresh, and usage for `uv`, `yt-dlp`, FFmpeg, and Deno |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| Current local work in Claude/Codex cloud | `setup/github-sync/README.md`; private `codex/local-sync/<machine>/<checkout>` branches and generated `.cloud-sync/` metadata/conversation exports. Cloud edits use separate task branches. |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — intake queue; `ingest` copies and hash-verifies clips into `raw/sources/clips/`; processed originals stay until removal is authorized, so inbox presence alone does not mean unprocessed |
| Application code | `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `../ai-os-projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

Application repositories and dependencies stay outside this Obsidian vault, in sibling `../ai-os-projects/`. Do not create a junction from the vault back into that directory.

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**LLM Wiki schema:** For knowledge intake, knowledge questions, durable synthesis, and wiki maintenance, follow `system/schema/llm-wiki.md`. Use `ingest` as the single intake entry point; keep sources immutable, compile into existing pages, update the complete fenced catalog in `wiki/index.md`, and append changes to `wiki/log.md`. Preserve domain homes and live-system authority. Mechanical tasks retain the retrieval exceptions above.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **LSAT teaching:** use project-owned `lsat-u-voice` and `references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md`; this Joshua-selected standard takes precedence over older vendor voice blends.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Shared Windows command tools:** use `uv`, `yt-dlp`, `ffmpeg`, `ffprobe`, `ffplay`, and `deno` from the Windows user PATH. For versions, locations, how to invoke them, or a stale terminal environment, read `references/ai-workspace/windows-cli-toolchain.md`. Check before reinstalling; restart the affected IDE when its inherited PATH is stale.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **Git remote is enabled.** `origin` is GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/ai-os`, decided 2026-09-29). The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.
- **New laptop restore:** GitHub contains tracked ai-os files and skills. It deliberately excludes `.env`, `raw/`, app repos, credentials, and machine sessions. Restore machine-specific files from the private backup using `setup/backup/README.md` and its `README - START HERE.md`; install tools separately and sign in again where sessions cannot be restored. Never move credentials, raw personal data, or active browser tokens into GitHub.
- **Authorized cloud transfer (2026-10-07):** private sync branches also carry portable skills, memory and conversation exports. Joshua explicitly authorized credential transfer; implementation uses the authenticated encrypted package described in `setup/github-sync/README.md`; plaintext secrets and its unlock key stay outside GitHub and Drive. Cloud authentication and native browser sessions still require separate verification.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `transcript-intake` | retrieving, filing or reconciling student/session transcripts; separate from knowledge `ingest` |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex and Claude both activate the LLM Wiki ingest, query, and lint workflows. Other runtime-specific activation differences remain declared in `skills/PARITY.md`. Never have separate runtimes writing the same page concurrently.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

</INSTRUCTIONS>

Sentence ends after \\"Dr/Mr/Mrs/Ms\\" or a single letter do not close a section.\\r\\n- Words = split(\\" \\"); id 1-based; charPosition.\\r\\n- Progress % = round((sectionIndex + posInSection/sectionLen) / sections * 100) (1 when on the last word of a section).\\r\\n- Remaining = time from current word to end: \\"N hour(s) and M minute(s)\\", \\"Less than a minute\\".\\r\\n\\r\\n### Layout (Serial / Flow / Highlight / Scroll)\\r\\n- Words are measured in the font; a line takes words while `x + width + space <= stageWidth - 40` **and** words < Words Per Line.\\r\\n- Serial: slide = Number of Lines lines, block vertically centred; Center Text centres each line, else x=20.\\r\\n- Flow: forced 1 line, spacing 1, left-aligned x=20; each chunk revealed by a bg-coloured mask sweeping left→right across the window over the chunk\'s duration (blank when paused at chunk start).\\r\\n- Highlight: page = floor((height-20)/fontSize) lines, left x=20, all visible in unhighlighted colour; current group (Number of Lines lines) drawn in font colour, not bold.\\r\\n- Scroll: same page, only current group visible at its own y; previous group hidden; new page starts at top.\\r\\n- Font size clamp 8..74. Line spacing multiplies fontSize.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Timing (shipped code)\\r\\n- Section total T = round(words / wpm * 60000). Chunks n. Even delay = T / (n - 1 + f), last = even*f where f = min(lastChunkLines / numberOfLines, 1).\\r\\n- Speed Variability: delay = round(T/n) * (chunkChars + (notLast?1:0)) / (sectionChars/n).\\r\\n- Punctuation Pauses: delay *= 2 if chunk text contains any of `. ? ! : ; - – ,` (any group size).\\r\\n- WPM clamp 50..5000, step 10 (keys ↑/+ and ↓/-), rounded to 10.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Dialogs\\r\\n- Preferences: 550x599 centred, radius 15, padding 14. \\"./ViewPanel\\";\\r\\nimport { Icon, MIcon } from \\"./Icon\\";\\r\\n\\r\\nconst GAP = 48;\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Spreeder e-Reader: the passage as one stream in a ~734px column, paged with\\r\\n * CSS columns. Words turn #3e92d7 on hover and open word actions on click.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function EReader({\\r\\n  prefs,\\r\\n  fontStack,\\r\\n  words,\\r\\n  page,\\r\\n  onPageCount,\\r\\n  onPageWord,\\r\\n  highlight,\\r\\n  onWordClick,\\r\\n}: {\\r\\n  prefs: Preferences;\\r\\n  fontStack: string;\\r\\n  words: Word[];\\r\\n  page: number;\\r\\n  onPageCount: (n: number) => void;\\r\\n  /** First word index on the visible page (reading position). */\\r\\n  onPageWord: (i: number) => void;\\r\\n  highlight: { start: number; length: number }[];\\r\\n  onWordClick: (w: Word) => void;\\r\\n}): React.ReactElement {\\r\\n  const box = useRef<HTMLDivElement | null>(null);\\r\\n  const [width, setWidth] = useState(0);\\r\\n\\r\\n  useLayoutEffect(() => {\\r\\n    const el = box.current;\\r\\n    if (!el) return;\\r\\n    const ro = new ResizeObserver(() => setWidth(el.clientWidth));\\r\\n    ro.observe(el);\\r\\n    setWidth(el.clientWidth);\\r\\n    return () => ro.disconnect();\\r\\n  }, []);\\r\\n\\r\\n  useLayoutEffect(() => {\\r\\n    const el = box.current;\\r\\n    if (!el || width <= 0) return;\\r\\n    onPageCount(Math.max(1, Math.round((el.scrollWidth + GAP) / (width + GAP))));\\r\\n  }, [width, words, prefs.font_family, prefs.font_size, prefs.line_spacing, onPageCount]);\\r\\n\\r\\n  useLayoutEffect(() => {\\r\\n    const el = box.current;\\r\\n    if (!el || width <= 0) return;\\r\\n    el.scrollLeft = page * (width + GAP);\\r\\n    const first = [...el.querySelectorAll<HTMLElement>(\\"[data-word]\\")].find((s) => s.offsetLeft >= page * (width + GAP) - 1);\\r\\n    if (first) onPageWord(Number(first.dataset.word));\\r\\n  }, [page, width, prefs.font_family, prefs.font_size, prefs.line_spacing, onPageWord]);\\r\\n\\r\\n  const marked = useMemo(() => {\\r\\n    const set = new Set<number>();\\r\\n    for (const h of highlight) for (let k = 0; k < h.length; k++) set.add(h.start + k);\\r\\n    return set;\\r\\n  }, [highlight]);\\r\\n\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <div className=\\"mx-auto h-full w-full max-w-[750px] px-2 pt-7\\">\\r\\n      <div\\r\\n        ref={box}\\r\\n        id=\\"onePage\\"\\r\\n        className=\\"relative overflow-hidden\\"\\r\\n        onClick={(e) => {\\r\\n          const t = (e.target as HTMLElement).closest<HTMLElement>(\\"[data-word]\\");\\r\\n          if (t) onWordClick(words[Number(t.dataset.word)]);\\r\\n        }}\\r\\n        style={{\\r\\n          height: \\"calc(100vh - 119px)\\",\\r\\n          columnWidth: width > 0 ? `${width}px` : undefined,\\r\\n          columnGap: GAP,\\r\\n          columnFill: \\"auto\\",\\r\\n          fontFamily: fontStack,\\r\\n          fontSize: prefs.font_size,\\r\\n          lineHeight: `${prefs.font_size * prefs.line_spacing}px`,\\r\\n          color: \\"var(--rc-read)\\",\\r\\n        }}\\r\\n      >\\r\\n        {words.map((w) => (\\r\\n          <React.Fragment key={w.index}>\\r\\n            <span data-word={w.index} className=\\"cursor-pointer hover:text-[#3e92d7]\\" style={marked.has(w.index) ? { background: \\"#ffff00\\", color: \\"#000\\" } : undefined}>\\r\\n              {w.text}\\r\\n            </span>{\\" \\"}\\r\\n          </React.Fragment>\\r\\n        ))}\\r\\n      </div>\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Page of a word, measured from the rendered column layout. */\\r\\nexport function usePageOf(): (wordIndex: number) => number {\\r\\n  return useCallback((wordIndex: number) => {\\r\\n    const el = document.getElementById(\\"onePage\\");\\r\\n    const span = el?.querySelector<HTMLElement>(`[data-word=\\"${wordIndex}\\"]`);\\r\\n    if (!el || !span) return 0;\\r\\n    return Math.floor(span.offsetLeft / (el.clientWidth + GAP));\\r\\n  }, []);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function EReaderBar({ page, pageCount, onPage }: { page: number; \\"#bdbdbd\\" : \\"var(--rc-strong)\\" }}>\\r\\n        <MIcon name=\\"keyboard_arrow_right\\" size={30} />\\r\\n      </button>\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** \\"Search in this book\\": results grouped by page, match in bold. */\\r\\nexport function EReaderSearch({\\r\\n  open,\\r\\n  onClose,\\r\\n  words,\\r\\n  pageOf,\\r\\n  onSelect,\\r\\n}: {\\r\\n  open: boolean;\\r\\n  onClose: () => void;\\r\\n  words: Word[];\\r\\n  pageOf: (i: number) => number;\\r\\n  onSelect: (page: number, hits: { start: number; length: number }[]) => void;\\r\\n}): React.ReactElement | null {\\r\\n  const [query, setQuery] = useState(\\"\\");\\r\\n  const [submitted, setSubmitted] = useState(\\"\\");\\r\\n  const [selected, setSelected] = useState<number | null>(null);\\r\\n  const qLen = submitted.trim().split(/\\\\s+/).filter(Boolean).length;\\r\\n  const groups = useMemo(() => {\\r\\n    const m = findMatches(words, submitted);\\r\\n    const byPage = new Map<number, number[]>();\\r\\n    for (const i of m) byPage.set(pageOf(i), [...(byPage.get(pageOf(i)) ?? Worker timers are not clamped. Falls back to setTimeout.\\r\\n */\\r\\nconst SOURCE = `\\r\\nlet id = null;\\r\\nself.onmessage = (e) => {\\r\\n  if (id !== null) { clearTimeout(id); id = null; }\\r\\n  if (e.data && e.data.delay != null) {\\r\\n    const start = Date.now();\\r\\n    const tick = () => {\\r\\n      if (Date.now() - start >= e.data.delay) { id = null; self.postMessage(e.data.token); }\\r\\n      else id = setTimeout(tick, Math.min(4, e.data.delay));\\r\\n    };\\r\\n    id = setTimeout(tick, Math.max(0, e.data.delay - 4));\\r\\n  }\\r\\n};`;\\r\\n\\r\\nlet worker: Worker | null | undefined;\\r\\nlet nextToken = 1;\\r\\nconst callbacks = new Map<number, () => void>();\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction getWorker(): Worker | null {\\r\\n  if (worker !== undefined) return worker;\\r\\n  try {\\r\\n    worker = new Worker(URL.createObjectURL(new Blob([SOURCE], { type: \\"text/javascript\\" })));\\r\\n    worker.onmessage = (e: MessageEvent<number>) => {\\r\\n      const cb = callbacks.get(e.data);\\r\\n      callbacks.delete(e.data);\\r\\n      cb?.();\\r\\n    };\\r\\n  } catch {\\r\\n    worker = null;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return worker;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** One pending timer at a time (the reader only ever needs one). Returns a cancel function. */\\r\\nexport function startTimer(delay: number, cb: () => void): () => void {\\r\\n  const w = getWorker();\\r\\n  if (!w) {\\r\\n    const t = window.setTimeout(cb, delay);\\r\\n    return () => window.clearTimeout(t);\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  const token = nextToken++;\\r\\n  callbacks.clear();\\r\\n  callbacks.set(token, cb);\\r\\n  w.postMessage({ delay, token });\\r\\n  return () => {\\r\\n    if (callbacks.delete(token)) w.postMessage({ delay: null });\\r\\n  };\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': chunkDelays,\\r\\n  evenChunkDelays,\\r\\n  formatDuration,\\r\\n  highlightPages,\\r\\n  normalizeText,\\r\\n  progressPercent,\\r\\n  remainingMs,\\r\\n  sectionTime,\\r\\n  serialChunks,\\r\\n} from \\"./spreeder\\";\\r\\n\\r\\nconst mono = (t: string) => t.length * 10; // 10px per character, space included\\r\\n\\r\\ndescribe(\\"text model\\", () => {\\r\\n  it(\\"collapses paragraphs and swaps em dashes, as Spreeder does\\", () => {\\r\\n    expect(normalizeText(\\"One.\\\\n\\\\nTwo—three  four\\")).toBe(\\"One. Two-three four\\");\\r\\n  });\\r\\n\\r\\n  it(\\"keeps sentences under 2000 characters together\\", () => {\\r\\n    expect(breakToSections(\\"First one. Second one. Third one.\\")).toEqual([\\"First one. Second one. Third one.\\"]);\\r\\n  });\\r\\n\\r\\n  it(\\"does not close a section after Mr. or a single initial\\", () => {\\r\\n    const long = \\"x\\".repeat(1990);\\r\\n    const s = breakToSections(`${long} said Mr. Smith. Next.`);\\r\\n    expect(s[0]).toContain(\\"Mr. Smith.\\");\\r\\n  });\\r\\n\\r\\n  it(\\"splits the seed passage into sections of at most about 2400 characters\\", () => {\\r\\n    const m = buildTextModel(SEED_PASSAGE.body);\\r\\n    expect(m.words.length).toBe(SEED_PASSAGE.body.trim().split(/\\\\s+/).length);\\r\\n    for (const s of m.sections) expect(s.text.length).toBeLessThanOrEqual(2600);\\r\\n    expect(m.words[0].charPosition).toBe(0);\\r\\n  });\\r\\n});\\r\\n\\r\\ndescribe(\\"layout\\", () => {\\r\\n  const model = buildTextModel(\\"aa bb cc dd ee\\");\\r\\n  const words = model.sections[0].words;\\r\\n\\r\\n  it(\\"caps a line at words per line\\", () => {\\r\\n    const lines = buildLines(words, mono, 1000, 2);\\r\\n    expect(lines.map((l) => l.words.map((w) => w.word.text))).toEqual([[\\"aa\\", \\"bb\\"], [\\"cc\\", \\"dd\\"], [\\"ee\\"]]);\\r\\n  });\\r\\n\\r\\n  it(\\"wraps on measured width before the word cap\\", () => {\\r\\n    // each word is 30px with its space; 70px fits two\\r\\n    const lines = buildLines(words, mono, 70, 6);\\r\\n    expect(lines[0].words.length).toBe(2);\\r\\n  });\\r\\n\\r\\n  it(\\"groups lines into serial slides and highlight pages\\", () => {\\r\\n    const lines = buildLines(words, mono, 1000, 1);\\r\\n    expect(serialChunks(lines, 2).map((c) => c.text)).toEqual([\\"aa bb\\", \\"cc dd\\", \\"ee\\"]);\\r\\n    const pages = highlightPages(lines, 20 + 36 * 3, 36, 1); // 3 lines per page\\r\\n    expect(pages.map((p) => p.lines.length)).toEqual([3, 2]);\\r\\n  });\\r\\n});\\r\\n\\r\\ndescribe(\\"timing\\", () => {\\r\\n  it(\\"shortens the last chunk by how full it is\\", () => {\\r\\n    expect(evenChunkDelays(1000, 3, 1, 2)).toEqual({ even: 400, last: 200 });\\r\\n  });\\r\\n\\r\\n  it(\\"gives a 200 WPM single-word slide 300 ms, doubled after punctuation\\", () => {\\r\\n    const m = buildTextModel(\\"one two three four.\\");\\r\\n    const s = m.sections[0];\\r\\n    const chunks = serialChunks(buildLines(s.words, mono, 1000, 1), 1);\\r\\n    const d = chunkDelays(s, chunks, { wpm: 200, numberOfLines: 1, speedVariability: false, punctuationPauses: true });\\r\\n    expect(d).toEqual([300, 300, 300, 600]);\\r\\n  });\\r\\n\\r\\n  it(\\"does not pause on an ASCII hyphen (Spreeder\'s shipped regex), but does on an en dash\\", () => {\\r\\n    const m = buildTextModel(\\"fast-food one two–three four\\");\\r\\n    const s = m.sections[0];\\r\\n    const chunks = serialChunks(buildLines(s.words, mono, 1000, 1), 1);\\r\\n    const d = chunkDelays(s, chunks, { wpm: 200, numberOfLines: 1, speedVariability: false, punctuationPauses: true });\\r\\n    expect(d).toEqual([300, 300, 600, 300]);\\r\\n  });\\r\\n\\r\\n  it(\\"Speed Variability scales each chunk by its characters and keeps the section total\\", () => {\\r\\n    const m = buildTextModel(\\"a bbb cc dddd\\");\\r\\n    const s = m.sections[0];\\r\\n    const chunks = serialChunks(buildLines(s.words, mono, 1000, 1), 1);\\r\\n    const d = chunkDelays(s, chunks, { wpm: 200, numberOfLines: 1, speedVariability: true, punctuationPauses: false });\\r\\n    // Spreeder: round(T/n) * (chars + 1 unless last) / (sectionChars / n); T = 1200, n = 4, 13 chars.\\r\\n    [600 / 3.25, 1200 / 3.25, 900 / 3.25, 1200 / 3.25].forEach((v, i) => expect(d[i]).toBeCloseTo(v, 6));\\r\\n    expect(d.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0)).toBeCloseTo(1200, 6);\\r\\n  });\\r\\n\\r\\n  it(\\"formats remaining time like Spreeder\\", () => {\\r\\n    expect(formatDuration(30_000)).toBe(\\"Less than a minute\\");\\r\\n    expect(formatDuration(3 * 60_000)).toBe(\\"3 minutes\\");\\r\\n    expect(formatDuration(61 * 60_000)).toBe(\\"1 hour and 1 minute\\");\\r\\n  });\\r\\n\\r\\n  it(\\"estimates the seed at about 3 minutes at 200 WPM\\", () => {\\r\\n    const m = buildTextModel(SEED_PASSAGE.body);\\r\\n    const ms = remainingMs(m, 0, { wpm: 200, groupSize: 1, numberOfLines: 1, punctuationPauses: false });\\r\\n    expect(formatDuration(ms)).toBe(\\"2 minutes\\");\\r\\n    expect(sectionTime(\\"a b\\", { wpm: 60, groupSize: 1, numberOfLines: 1, punctuationPauses: false })).toBe(2000);\\r\\n  });\\r\\n\\r\\n  it(\\"reports progress by section\\", () => {\\r\\n    const m = buildTextModel(\\"A b. C d.\\");\\r\\n    expect(progressPercent(m, 0)).toBe(0);\\r\\n  });\\r\\n});\\r\\n"}'}]

Math.max(0, now - this.pausedSince) : 0);\n+    const result = this.startedAt != null && activeMs >= 5000 && this.wordsRead > 0 ? {\n+      words_read: this.wordsRead,\n+      active_ms: activeMs,\n+      paused_ms: pausedMs,\n+      started_at: new Date(this.startedAt).toISOString(),\n+      ended_at: new Date(now).toISOString(),\n+    } : null;\n+    this.startedAt = this.activeSince = this.pausedSince = null;\n+    this.activeMs = this.pausedMs = this.wordsRead = 0;\n+    return result;\n+  }\n+}\n*** Add File: C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/reading-center/src/features/reading-center/lib/session.test.ts\n+import { describe, expect, it } from \"vitest\";\n+import { ReadingVisit } from \"./session\";\n+\n+describe(\"reading visit statistics\", () => {\n+  it(\"counts the final chunk and active/paused intervals without including initial idle time\", () => {\n+    const visit = new ReadingVisit();\n+    visit.play(10000);\n+    visit.read(5);\n+    visit.pause(13000);\n+    visit.play(17000);\n+    visit.read(3);\n+    expect(visit.finish(21000)).toMatchObject({ words_read: 8, active_ms: 7000, paused_ms: 4000, started_at: new Date(10000).toISOString() });\n+    expect(visit.finish(22000)).toBeNull();\n+  });\n+  it(\"keeps a single fully displayed chunk and discards short or unread visits\", () => {\n+    const visit = new ReadingVisit();\n+    visit.play(0);\n+    visit.read(8);\n+    expect(visit.finish(6000)?.words_read).toBe(8);\n+    visit.play(7000);\n+    visit.read(1);\n+    expect(visit.finish(8000)).toBeNull();\n+    visit.play(9000);\n+    expect(visit.finish(16000)).toBeNull();\n+  });\n+  it(\"does not count a repeated play/pause notification twice\", () => {\n+    const visit = new ReadingVisit();\n+    visit.play(0);\n+    visit.play(1000);\n+    visit.read(4);\n+    visit.pause(6000);\n+    visit.pause(7000);\n+    expect(visit.finish(9000)).toMatchObject({ active_ms: 6000, paused_ms: 3000 });\n+  });\n+});\n*** End Patch"));

text(await tools.apply_patch("*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/reading-center/src/features/reading-center/hooks/useSpreederEngine.test.tsx\n+// @vitest-environment jsdom\n+import React, { act } from \"react\";\n+import { createRoot, type Root } from \"react-dom/client\";\n+import { afterEach, beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from \"vitest\";\n+import { useSpreederEngine } from \"./useSpreederEngine\";\n+import { DEFAULT_PREFERENCES } from \"../lib/constants\";\n+import { buildTextModel } from \"../lib/spreeder\";\n+\n+vi.mock(\"../lib/workerTimer\", () => ({ startTimer: (delay: number, callback: () => void) => {\n+  const timer = window.setTimeout(callback, delay);\n+  return () => window.clearTimeout(timer);\n+} }));\n+\n+(globalThis as { IS_REACT_ACT_ENVIRONMENT?: boolean }).IS_REACT_ACT_ENVIRONMENT = true;\n+const model = buildTextModel(\"one two\");\n+const measure = (text: string) => text.length * 10;\n+const read = vi.fn();\n+const finish = vi.fn();\n+let engine: ReturnType<typeof useSpreederEngine>;\n+function Harness() {\n+  engine = useSpreederEngine({ model, prefs: { ...DEFAULT_PREFERENCES, wpm: 200, words_per_line: 1, number_of_lines: 1, punctuation_pauses: false }, stage: { width: 500, height: 500 }, measure, initialWord: 0, onFinish: finish, onReadChunk: read });\n+  return null;\n+}\n+let root: Root;\n+let node: HTMLDivElement;\n+beforeEach(() => {\n+  vi.useFakeTimers({ toFake: [\"setTimeout\", \"clearTimeout\", \"Date\", \"performance\"] });\n+  read.mockClear(); finish.mockClear();\n+  node = document.createElement(\"div\"); document.body.append(node);\n+  root = createRoot(node);\n+  act(() => root.render(<Harness />));\n+});\n+afterEach(() => { act(() => root.unmount()); node.remove(); vi.useRealTimers(); });\n+\n+describe(\"reader playback\", () => {\n+  it(\"preserves remaining chunk time on pause/resume and counts the final chunk\", () => {\n+    act(() => engine.play());\n+    act(() => vi.advanceTimersByTime(200));\n+    act(() => engine.pause());\n+    act(() => vi.advanceTimersByTime(1000));\n+    expect(read).not.toHaveBeenCalled();\n+    act(() => engine.play());\n+    act(() => vi.advanceTimersByTime(99));\n+    expect(read).not.toHaveBeenCalled();\n+    act(() => vi.advanceTimersByTime(1));\n+    expect(read).toHaveBeenCalledWith(1);\n+    expect(engine.activeWord).toBe(1);\n+    act(() => vi.advanceTimersByTime(300));\n+    expect(read).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(2);\n+    expect(finish).toHaveBeenCalledOnce();\n+    expect(engine.playing).toBe(false);\n+  });\n+  it(\"does not treat manual navigation as words read\", () => {\n+    act(() => engine.next());\n+    expect(engine.activeWord).toBe(1);\n+    expect(read).not.toHaveBeenCalled();\n+    act(() => engine.restartSection());\n+    expect(engine.activeWord).toBe(0);\n+    expect(engine.seekVersion).toBe(2);\n+  });\n+});\n*** End Patch"));

## assistant

Implemented standalone fixes in `reading-center/src/features/reading-center`:

- Flow stays masked while paused, freezes during playback pause, resumes the remaining chunk time, and resets on navigation/restart.
- Completion persists when reading finishes. Library now includes a Finished shelf.
- eReader exposes **Mark finished** on the final page.
- Session statistics include the final chunk and actual active/paused time.
- Import validates supported formats, size, empty/binary contents, and read errors; HTML excludes scripts/styles and preserves block spacing. Added a retry chooser.
- Fullscreen failures produce explanatory messages.

Validation: **38 tests passed**, TypeScript passed, production build passed. Build retains the existing large bundle warning. No commits or pushes.

Changed: `AddText.tsx`, `EReader.tsx`, `SpreederStage.tsx`, `useSpreederEngine.ts`, `LibraryPage.tsx`, `ReaderPage.tsx`. Added `importText.ts`, `session.ts`, and regression tests.

Browser verification remains with the parent. Files are stable and ready for integration copying.

Body uses $rc$ quoting so apostrophes need no escaping.\\r\\ninsert into public.reading_center_passages (slug, title, author, body, word_count, sort_order)\\r\\nvalues (\\r\\n  \'living-in-a-rational-society\',\\r\\n  \'Living in a rational society\',\\r\\n  null,\\r\\n  $rc$The rationalizing of society can be conceptualized as the pursuit of efficiency, predictability, calculability, and control through technology. But rational systems inevitably spawn a series of irrationalities that result in the compromising and perhaps even the undermining of their rationality. Fast-food restaurants, which epitomize the rational model, proffer the fastest means of getting from a hungry state to a sated one, without surprises, at low cost, in a carnival-like setting suggesting that fun awaits the consumer at each visit. The wholesomeness of the food seems an insignificant consideration. Whereas in the past, working people were prepared to spend up to an hour preparing dinner, they now are impatient if a meal is not on the table within ten minutes. (For their part, some fast-food restaurants have developed chairs that become uncomfortable after about twenty minutes, to ensure that diners do not stay long.) Fast-food restaurants have preferentially recruited adolescent help, at least until recently, because this age group adjusts more easily than adults do to surrendering their autonomy to machines, rules, and procedures. Few skills are required on the job, so workers are asked to use only a minute portion of their abilities. This policy is irrational from the standpoint of the organization, since it could obtain much more from its employees for the money (however negligible) it pays them. These minimal skill demands are also irrational from the perspective of the employees, who are not allowed to think or to respond creatively to the demands of the work. These restrictions lead to high levels of resentment, job dissatisfaction, alienation, absenteeism, and turnover among workers in fast-food franchises. In fact, these businesses have the highest turnover rate of any industry in the U.S. The entire workforce of the fast-food industry turns over three times in a year. Although the simple, repetitive nature of the work makes it easy to replace those who leave, the organization would clearly benefit from keeping employees longer. The costs of hiring and training are magnified when the turnover rate is extraordinarily high.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe application of the rational model to the house-building process in the 1950s and ’60s led to suburban communities consisting of nearly identical structures. Indeed, it was possible to wander into the residence of someone else and not to realize immediately that one was not at home. The more expensive developments were superficially more diversified, but their interior layouts assumed residents who were indistinguishable in their requirements. Furthermore, the planned communities themselves look very similar. Established trees are bulldozed to facilitate construction. In their place, a number of saplings, held up by posts and wire, are planted. Streets are laid out in symmetrical grid patterns. With such uniformity, suburbanites may well enter the wrong subdivision or become lost in their own.\\r\\n\\r\\nMany of Steven Spielberg’s films are set in such suburbs. Spielberg’s strategy is to lure the viewer into this highly repetitive world and then to have a completely unexpected event occur. For example, the film Poltergeist takes place in a conventional suburban household in which evil spirits ultimately disrupt the sameness. (The spirits first manifest themselves through another key element of the homogeneous society—the television set.) The great success of Spielberg’s films may be traceable to a longing for some unpredictability, even if it is bizarre and menacing, in increasingly routinized lives.$rc$,\\r\\n  0,\\r\\n  1\\r\\n)\\r\\non conflict (slug) do nothing;\\r\\n\\r\\n-- word_count for the seed: count whitespace-separated words.\\r\\nupdate public.reading_center_passages\\r\\nset word_count = array_length(regexp_split_to_array(trim(body), \'\\\\s+\'), 1)\\r\\nwhere slug = \'living-in-a-rational-society\';\\r\\n"}}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"status":"fulfilled","value":{"chunk_id":"d425ae","wall_time_seconds":0.5041976,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":3158,"output":"-- Whole-tool access is issued through immutable Classroom source fields.\\r\\n-- Re-runnable; no existing student reading data is removed.\\r\\nbegin;\\r\\n\\r\\ncreate or replace function public.has_reading_center_access()\\r\\nreturns boolean language sql stable security definer set search_path = public\\r\\nas $$\\r\\n  select auth.uid() is not null and (\\r\\n    public.has_role(auth.uid(), \'admin\') or exists (\\r\\n      select 1 from public.classroom_items ci\\r\\n      where ci.student_id = auth.uid() and ci.type = \'activity\'\\r\\n        and ci.source_table = \'reading_center\' and ci.source_id = \'library\'\\r\\n        and ci.visible_to_student and ci.status in (\'assigned\',\'in_progress\',\'completed\')\\r\\n        and (ci.unlock_at is null or ci.unlock_at <= now())\\r\\n    )\\r\\n  );\\r\\n$$;\\r\\nrevoke all on function public.has_reading_center_access() from public, anon;\\r\\ngrant execute on function public.has_reading_center_access() to authenticated;\\r\\n\\r\\n-- Classroom allows students to advance status. A draft or archived grant must\\r\\n-- never become an entitlement through that ordinary progress update path.\\r\\ncreate or replace function public.guard_reading_center_assignment_update()\\r\\nreturns trigger language plpgsql security definer set search_path=public\\r\\nas $$\\r\\nbegin\\r\\n  if auth.uid() is not null and not public.has_role(auth.uid(),\'admin\') and\\r\\n    (old.source_table=\'reading_center\' or new.source_table=\'reading_center\') then\\r\\n    if new.source_table is distinct from old.source_table or new.source_id is distinct from old.source_id\\r\\n      or new.student_id is distinct from old.student_id or new.created_by is distinct from old.created_by\\r\\n      or new.type is distinct from old.type or new.visible_to_student is distinct from old.visible_to_student\\r\\n      or new.unlock_at is distinct from old.unlock_at\\r\\n      or old.status not in (\'assigned\',\'in_progress\',\'completed\')\\r\\n      or new.status not in (\'assigned\',\'in_progress\',\'completed\') then\\r\\n      raise exception \'Only an administrator may change Reading Center access\' using errcode=\'42501\';\\r\\n    end if;\\r\\n  end if;\\r\\n  return new;\\r\\nend;\\r\\n$$;\\r\\nrevoke all on function public.guard_reading_center_assignment_update() from public,anon,authenticated;\\r\\ndrop trigger if exists reading_center_assignment_update_guard on public.classroom_items;\\r\\ncreate trigger reading_center_assignment_update_guard before update on public.classroom_items\\r\\nfor each row execute function public.guard_reading_center_assignment_update();\\r\\n\\r\\n-- This partial unique index plus an advisory transaction lock prevents double\\r\\n-- assignments from two tabs. Revocation preserves the previous item as history.\\r\\ncreate unique index if not exists reading_center_one_active_assignment\\r\\non public.classroom_items (student_id)\\r\\nwhere source_table = \'reading_center\' and source_id = \'library\'\\r\\n  and visible_to_student and status in (\'assigned\',\'in_progress\',\'completed\');\\r\\n\\r\\ncreate or replace function public.assign_reading_center(\\r\\n  p_student_id uuid, p_unlock_at timestamptz default null,\\r\\n  p_title text default \'Reading Center\',\\r\\n  p_description text default \'Practice reading articles with five reading modes, bookmarks and reading goals.\'\\r\\n) returns jsonb language plpgsql security definer set search_path = public\\r\\nas $$\\r\\ndeclare v_item public.classroom_items%rowtype;\\r\\nbegin\\r\\n  if auth.uid() is null or not public.has_role(auth.uid(),\'admin\') then\\r\\n    raise exception \'Only an administrator may assign Reading Center\' using errcode = \'42501\';\\r\\n  end if;\\r\\n  if not exists (select 1 from auth.users where id = p_student_id) then\\r\\n    raise exception \'Student account not found\' using errcode = \'22023\';\\r\\n  end if;\\r\\n  perform pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended(\'reading-center:\' || p_student_id::text, 0));\\r\\n  select * into v_item from public.classroom_items\\r\\n    where student_id = p_student_id and type = \'activity\'\\r\\n      and source_table = \'reading_center\' and source_id = \'library\'\\r\\n      and visible_to_student and status in An\\r\\n  // unreadable student level belongs in the same block for the same reason: the\\r\\n  // catch below reports every student as failed and nothing has been written.\\r\\n  let plans: AssignPlan[];\\r\\n  try {\\r\\n    // Only guided drills carry a level, so nothing else pays for the lookup.\\r\\n    const needsLevels = items.some((item) => item.kind === \'guided_drill\');\\r\\n    const levels: StudentLevelMap = needsLevels\\r\\n      ? await fetchStudentLevels(studentIds)\\r\\n      : new Map();\\r\\n\\r\\n    plans = await Promise.all(\\r\\n      items.map(async (item, index) => {\\r\\n        const base = await planFor(\\r\\n          item,\\r\\n          teacherId,\\r\\n          scheduledForLocalDate,\\r\\n          levels,\\r\\n          levelOverrides,\\r\\n          questionCountOverride,\\r\\n          frozenQidsByStudent,\\r\\n        );\\r\\n        // Overrides only ever accompany a single item (see assignWork).\\r\\n        if (index > 0 || !overrides) return base;\\r\\n        return {\\r\\n          ...base,\\r\\n          title: overrides.title?.trim() || base.title,\\r\\n          description: overrides.description?.trim() || base.description,\\r\\n          dueAt: overrides.dueAt !== undefined ? overrides.dueAt : base.dueAt,\\r\\n          metadata: { ...base.metadata, ...(overrides.metadata ?? {}) },\\r\\n        };\\r\\n      }),\\r\\n    );\\r\\n  } catch (error) {\\r\\n    // Nothing was written, so every student failed for the same reason.\\r\\n    const reason = message(error, \'Could not load the item to assign\');\\r\\n    return { ok, failed: studentIds.map((studentId) => ({ studentId, error: reason })) };\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  for (const studentId of studentIds) {\\r\\n    const written: Written[] = [];\\r\\n\\r\\n    try {\\r\\n      const routes: string[] = [];\\r\\n\\r\\n      for (let i = 0; i < plans.length; i++) {\\r\\n        const plan = plans[i];\\r\\n        const record: Written = {\\r\\n          assignmentId: null,\\r\\n          classroomItemId: null,\\r\\n          table: plan.sourceTable,\\r\\n          kind: items[i].kind,\\r\\n        };\\r\\n        // Recorded before each write so a throw mid-item still rolls back what\\r\\n        // the previous items wrote.\\r\\n        written.push(record);\\r\\n\\r\\n        record.assignmentId = await plan.insertRow(studentId);\\r\\n        const route = plan.route(record.assignmentId);\\r\\n        routes.push(route);\\r\\n\\r\\n        const classroomItem = await createClassroomItem({\\r\\n          studentId,\\r\\n          createdBy: teacherId,\\r\\n          type: plan.type,\\r\\n          title: plan.title,\\r\\n          description: plan.description,\\r\\n          sourceTable: plan.sourceTable,\\r\\n          sourceId: plan.sourceId(record.assignmentId),\\r\\n          unlockAt: plan.unlockAt,\\r\\n          dueAt: plan.dueAt,\\r\\n          visibleToStudent: true,\\r\\n          status: \'assigned\',\\r\\n          metadata: { ...plan.metadata, assigned_route: route },\\r\\n        });\\r\\n        record.classroomItemId = classroomItem.id;\\r\\n      }\\r\\n\\r\\n      // A caller that owns its own messaging opts out. The weekly-plan builder\\r\\n      // does: it sends one summary for the whole week, and seven bell entries\\r\\n      // for one plan is the exact UX it already avoids.\\r\\n      const summary = notificationFor(plans, routes);\\r\\n      const notify = shouldNotify\\r\\n        ? await notifyStudentOfClassroomItem({\\r\\n            adminUserId: teacherId,\\r\\n            studentId,\\r\\n            title: summary.title,\\r\\n            description: summary.description,\\r\\n            route: summary.route,\\r\\n            assignmentType: summary.assignmentType,\\r\\n          })\\r\\n        : undefined;\\r\\n\\r\\n      ok.push({\\r\\n        studentId,\\r\\n        assignmentId: written[0]?.assignmentId ?? undefined,\\r\\n        classroomItemId: written[0]?.classroomItemId ?? undefined,\\r\\n        route: routes[0],\\r\\n        assignmentIds: written.map((w) => w.assignmentId).filter((id): id is string => !!id),\\r\\n        classroomItemIds: written\\r\\n          .map((w) => w.classroomItemId)\\r\\n          .filter((id): id is string => !!id),\\r\\n        notify,\\r\\n      });\\r\\n    } catch (error) {\\r\\n      console.error(\'[assignWork] student failed\', {\\r\\n        studentId,\\r\\n        kinds: items.map((i) => i.kind),\\r\\n        written,\\r\\n        error,\\r\\n      });\\r\\n      // Newest first, so the half-written item goes before the ones that landed.\\r\\n      for (const record of [...written].reverse()) {\\r\\n        await rollback(record);\\r\\n      }\\r\\n      failed.push({ studentId, error: message(error, \'Assign failed\') });\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  return { ok, failed };\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * One handoff, one message. A single item keeps its own wording; a bundle is\\r\\n * announced as a list pointing at the Classroom, since no one route covers it.\\r\\n */\\r\\nfunction notificationFor(\\r\\n  plans: AssignPlan[],\\r\\n  routes: string[],\\r\\n): { title: string; description?: string; route: string; assignmentType?: string } {\\r\\n  if (plans.length === 1) {\\r\\n    return {\\r\\n      title: plans[0].title,\\r\\n      description: plans[0].description,\\r\\n      route: routes[0],\\r\\n      assignmentType: plans[0].assignmentType,\\r\\n    };\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return {\\r\\n    title: `${plans.length} new pieces of work`,\\r\\n    description: plans.map((p) => `• ${p.title}`).join(\'\\\\n\'),\\r\\n    route: \'/classroom\',\\r\\n    assignmentType: \'assignment\',\\r\\n  };\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * One sentence for the teacher, in the order they care about: who missed out,\\r\\n * then which messages did not go, then plain success. Shared so every assign\\r\\n * surface reports the same outcome the same way.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function describeAssignResult(\\r\\n  result: AssignWorkResult,\\r\\n  title: string,\\r\\n): { level: \'success\' | \'warning\' | \'error\'; message: string } {\\r\\n  const { ok, failed } = result;\\r\\n  const students = (n: number) => `${n} student${n === 1 ? \'\' : \'s\'}`;\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (ok.length === 0) {\\r\\n    return {\\r\\n      level: \'error\',\\r\\n      message: failed[0]?.error\\r\\n        ? `Could not assign \\"${title}\\": ${failed[0].error}`\\r\\n        : `Could not assign \\"${title}\\"`,\\r\\n    };\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (failed.length > 0) {\\r\\n    return {\\r\\n      level: \'warning\',\\r\\n      message: `Assigned \\"${title}\\" to ${students(ok.length)}. ${students(failed.length)} failed: ${failed[0].error ?? \'unknown error\'}`,\\r\\n    };\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  const quiet = ok.filter((r) => r.notify && !(r.notify.inbox_ok && r.notify.bell_ok && r.notify.email_ok));\\r\\n  if (quiet.length > 0) {\\r\\n    return {\\r\\n      level: \'warning\',\\r\\n      message: `Assigned \\"${title}\\" to ${students(ok.length)}, but ${students(quiet.length)} were not fully notified.`,\\r\\n    };\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  return { level: \'success\', message: `Assigned \\"${title}\\" to ${students(ok.length)}` };\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Undo a half-written assign. Best effort: if the cleanup itself fails there is\\r\\n * nothing further to try, so it is logged loudly and swallowed rather than\\r\\n * masking the original error that sent us here.\\r\\n */\\r\\nasync function rollback(input: {\\r\\n  assignmentId: string | null;\\r\\n  classroomItemId: string | null;\\r\\n  table: string;\\r\\n  kind: AssignableItem[\'kind\'];\\r\\n}) {\\r\\n  const { assignmentId, classroomItemId, table, kind } = input;\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (classroomItemId) {\\r\\n    const { error } = await supabase.from(\'classroom_items\').delete().eq(\'id\', classroomItemId);\\r\\n    if (error) console.error(\'[assignWork] rollback left a classroom item\', { classroomItemId, error });\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  // Bootcamps write no assignment row, so there is nothing else to undo.\\r\\n  if (!assignmentId || kind === \'bootcamp\') return;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const { error } = await supabase\\r\\n    .from(table as \'homework_assignments\' | \'guided_drill_assignments\' | \'activity_assignments\')\\r\\n    .delete()\\r\\n    .eq(\'id\', assignmentId);\\r\\n  if (error) console.error(\'[assignWork] rollback left an assignment row\', { table, assignmentId, error });\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}}'}]

Passage } from \\"./types\\";\\r\\n\\r\\nexport interface PassageMetadata {\\r\\n  passage_id: string;\\r\\n  tags?: string[];\\r\\n  hidden?: boolean;\\r\\n  title_override?: string | null;\\r\\n  body_override?: string | null;\\r\\n  author_override?: string | null;\\r\\n  genre_override?: string | null;\\r\\n  cover_url_override?: string | null;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Null means no override; empty optional fields deliberately clear the original. */\\r\\nexport function mergePassageMetadata(passage: Passage, metadata?: PassageMetadata): Passage | null {\\r\\n  if (metadata?.hidden) return null;\\r\\n  if (!metadata) return { ...passage, tags: [] };\\r\\n  const body = metadata.body_override ?? passage.body;\\r\\n  return {\\r\\n    ...passage,\\r\\n    title: metadata.title_override ?? passage.title,\\r\\n    body,\\r\\n    word_count: countWords(body),\\r\\n    author: metadata.author_override == null ? passage.author : metadata.author_override || null,\\r\\n    genre: metadata.genre_override == null ? passage.genre : metadata.genre_override || null,\\r\\n    cover_url: metadata.cover_url_override == null ? passage.cover_url : metadata.cover_url_override || null,\\r\\n    tags: metadata.tags ?? [],\\r\\n  };\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function metadataForEdit(input: Partial<Passage>): Partial<PassageMetadata> {\\r\\n  const result: Partial<PassageMetadata> = {};\\r\\n  for (const field of [\\"title\\", \\"body\\", \\"author\\", \\"genre\\", \\"cover_url\\"] as const) {\\r\\n    if (Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(input, field)) result[`${field}_override`] = input[field] ?? \\"\\";\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  if (input.tags) result.tags = input.tags;\\r\\n  return result;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function progressPayload(userId: string, input: { passage_id: string; word_index: number; completed?: boolean; hidden_from_recent?: boolean }) {\\r\\n  const now = new Date().toISOString();\\r\\n  const payload: Record<string, unknown> = {\\r\\n    user_id: userId,\\r\\n    passage_id: input.passage_id,\\r\\n    word_index: Math.max(0, Math.floor(input.word_index)),\\r\\n    last_opened_at: now,\\r\\n  };\\r\\n  if (input.completed !== undefined) payload.completed_at = input.completed ? now : null;\\r\\n  if (input.hidden_from_recent !== undefined) payload.hidden_from_recent = input.hidden_from_recent;\\r\\n  return payload;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport const readingCenterKey = (userId: string | undefined, ...parts: unknown[]) => [\\"reading-center\\", userId, ...parts];\\r\\n"}'}]

[]) as ClassroomItem[];\\r\\n    },\\r\\n  });\\r\\n  const current = assignments.data?.[0];\\r\\n  const refresh = async () => {\\r\\n    await Promise.all([\\r\\n      cache.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: [\'reading-center-assignment-admin\', studentId] }),\\r\\n      cache.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: [\'classroom-items-admin\', studentId] }),\\r\\n      cache.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: [\'classroom-items\', studentId] }),\\r\\n      cache.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: [\'reading-center-access\', studentId] }),\\r\\n    ]);\\r\\n  };\\r\\n  const assign = async () => {\\r\\n    if (!user || !studentId) return;\\r\\n    setBusy(true); setFeedback(\'\');\\r\\n    try {\\r\\n      const result = await assignWork({ teacherId: user.id, studentIds: [studentId], item: { kind: \'reading_center\' },\\r\\n        scheduledForLocalDate: scheduled || undefined });\\r\\n      if (result.failed.length) throw new Error(result.failed[0].error);\\r\\n      await refresh();\\r\\n      setFeedback(result.ok[0]?.notify && !Object.values(result.ok[0].notify).every(Boolean)\\r\\n        ? \'Reading Center is assigned and visible in the student’s work. A notification did not arrive.\'\\r\\n        : scheduled\\r\\n          ? `Reading Center assignment verified. It will appear in the student’s work from ${scheduled}.`\\r\\n          : \'Reading Center assignment verified. The student can open it from their assigned work.\');\\r\\n    } catch (error) { setFeedback(error instanceof Error ? error.message : \'Assignment failed.\'); }\\r\\n    finally { setBusy(false); }\\r\\n  };\\r\\n  const revoke = async () => {\\r\\n    setBusy(true); setFeedback(\'\');\\r\\n    try { await revokeReadingCenter(studentId); await refresh(); setFeedback(\'Reading Center access removed. Saved reading work is preserved.\'); }\\r\\n    catch (error) { setFeedback(error instanceof Error ? error.message : \'Could not remove access.\'); }\\r\\n    finally { setBusy(false); }\\r\\n  };\\r\\n\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"mx-auto w-full max-w-3xl space-y-6 px-5 py-6 sm:px-6\\">\\r\\n    <div><h1 className=\\"text-2xl font-semibold\\">Assign Reading Center</h1>\\r\\n      <p className=\\"mt-2 text-sm text-black/65\\">Give a student access to the reading tool. It appears in their assigned work when its start date arrives.</p></div>\\r\\n    <Link className=\\"inline-block rounded border border-black/20 px-4 py-2 text-sm\\" to={READING_CENTER_PATH}>Open Reading Center</Link>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"space-y-4 rounded border border-black/15 bg-white/30 p-5\\">\\r\\n      <label className=\\"block text-sm font-medium\\">Student\\r\\n        <select className=\\"mt-2 block w-full rounded border border-black/20 bg-white p-2\\" value={studentId}\\r\\n          onChange={(event) => { setStudentId(event.target.value); setFeedback(\'\'); }} disabled={studentsLoading || busy}>\\r\\n          <option value=\\"\\">Choose a student</option>\\r\\n          {students.filter((student) => student.role !== \'admin\').map((student) => <option key={student.class_id} value={student.class_id}>\\r\\n            {student.display_name || student.email || student.class_id}{student.display_name && student.email ? ` (${student.email})` : \'\'}\\r\\n          </option>)}\\r\\n        </select>\\r\\n      </label>\\r\\n      {studentsError && <p role=\\"alert\\">Could not load students: {studentsError.message}</p>}\\r\\n      {assignments.isError && <p role=\\"alert\\">Could not check this student’s current access. <button className=\\"underline\\" onClick={() => void assignments.refetch()}>Try again</button></p>}\\r\\n      {current ? <div className=\\"space-y-3\\"><p className=\\"text-sm\\">{current.unlock_at && new Date(current.unlock_at) > new Date()\\r\\n        ? `Assigned. Available from ${new Date(current.unlock_at).toLocaleDateString(\'en-CA\')}.`\\r\\n        : \'Reading Center is assigned to this student.\'}</p>\\r\\n        <button className=\\"rounded border border-black/25 px-4 py-2 text-sm\\" disabled={busy} onClick={() => void revoke()}>Remove access</button>\\r\\n      </div> : <>\\r\\n        <label className=\\"block text-sm font-medium\\">Start date (optional)\\r\\n          <input type=\\"date\\" className=\\"mt-2 block rounded border border-black/20 bg-white p-2\\" value={scheduled} onChange={(event) => setScheduled(event.target.value)} disabled={busy} />\\r\\n        </label>\\r\\n        <button className=\\"rounded bg-black px-4 py-2 text-sm text-white disabled:opacity-40\\" onClick={() => void assign()}\\r\\n          disabled={!studentId || busy || assignments.isPending || assignments.isError}>{busy ? \'Assigning…\' : \'Assign Reading Center\'}</button>\\r\\n      </>}\\r\\n      {feedback && <p role=\\"status\\" className=\\"text-sm\\">{feedback}</p>}\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  </div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}]

## assistant

Two deployment blockers found:

1. **Completion reports success before the cloud save succeeds.** `ReaderPage.tsx:140` calls `saveProgress.mutate()` and immediately shows Congratulations. Offline or denied writes leave completion unsaved, while the alert permits navigation and suppresses retries. Await confirmed persistence and retain a retry path.

2. **Tags query a nonexistent column.** `useReadingCenterData.ts` orders `reading_center_tags` by `created_at`, but the migration defines only `user_id`, `name`, and `color`. Every Tags query fails. Change ordering or add the column.

Also fix affected-row verification for bookmark updates: the hook currently treats an RLS-filtered zero-row update as success and displays **Bookmark saved**.

No student access bypass or cross-account leak found in the reviewed combined policies. Assignment RPC authorization, immutable grant fields, advisory locking, unique assignment index, and second-read verification are consistent. This was static review, not live database acceptance.
